We must show that there exists
0\leq p=\sum_{j}(k_{j}-k^{\prime}_{j})+\sum_{i}(e_{i}+\boldsymbol{\delta}^{\bot}_{\mathsf{d}_{i},\bullet})
such that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}&&\langle\Gamma^{\prime},{\mathsf{M}}^{*}_{1}\xrightarrow{{\bullet}}\cdots{\mathsf{M}}^{*}_{n}\xrightarrow{{\bullet}}{\mathsf{M}}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{1}\xrightarrow{{\mathsf{d}_{1}}}\cdots{\mathsf{M}}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{r}\xrightarrow{{\mathsf{d}_{r}}}{\mathsf{L}}_{0}\rangle\\
&\leq^{+}_{p}&\langle\Gamma,{\mathsf{M}}_{1}\xrightarrow{{\bullet}}\cdots{\mathsf{M}}_{n}\xrightarrow{{\bullet}}{\mathsf{M}}^{\prime}_{1}\xrightarrow{{\bullet}}\cdots{\mathsf{M}}^{\prime}_{r}\xrightarrow{{\bullet}}{\mathsf{L}}_{0}\rangle\\
\end{array}

We need to perform some calculations. We have:

\sum_{j}\Delta^{\prime}_{j}\leq^{-}_{(\sum_{j}d^{1}_{j})}\sum_{j}\Delta_{j}\qquad\textrm{ and
}\qquad{\mathsf{L}}_{2}\leq^{-}_{(\sum_{j}d^{2}_{j}+\sum_{i}e^{\prime\prime}_{i})}{\mathsf{L}}_{1}

Since
\Gamma^{\prime},\vec{x}:\vec{\mathsf{M}}^{*}=y:[{\mathsf{L}}_{2}]+\sum_{j}\Delta^{\prime}_{j}
and

\Gamma,\vec{x}:\vec{\mathsf{M}}=y:[{\mathsf{L}}_{1}]+\sum_{j}\Delta_{j}
and
\sum_{j}d_{j}=\sum_{j}(d^{1}_{j}+d^{2}_{j})
, we get:

\Gamma^{\prime},\vec{x}:\vec{\mathsf{M}}^{*}\leq^{-}_{\sum_{j}d_{j}+\sum_{i}e^{\prime\prime}_{i}}\Gamma,\vec{x}:\vec{\mathsf{M}}

(4)

From
{\mathsf{M}}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{i}\leq^{-}_{e^{\prime}_{i}}{\mathsf{M}}^{\prime}_{i}
, we get:

{\mathsf{M}}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{1}\xrightarrow{{\mathsf{d}_{1}}}\cdots{\mathsf{M}}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{r}\xrightarrow{{\mathsf{d}_{r}}}{\mathsf{L}}_{0}\leq^{+}_{\sum_{i}(\boldsymbol{\delta}^{\bot}_{\mathsf{d}_{i},\bullet}+e^{\prime}_{i})}{\mathsf{M}}^{\prime}_{1}\xrightarrow{{\bullet}}\cdots{\mathsf{M}}^{\prime}_{r}\xrightarrow{{\bullet}}{\mathsf{L}}_{0}

(5)

Therefore, we are going to take

\displaystyle p

\displaystyle:=(\sum_{j}d_{j})+\sum_{i}e^{\prime\prime}_{i}+\sum_{i}(\boldsymbol{\delta}^{\bot}_{\mathsf{d}_{i},\bullet}+e^{\prime}_{i})=\sum_{j}d_{j}+\sum_{i}(e_{i}+\boldsymbol{\delta}^{\bot}_{\mathsf{d}_{i},\bullet})

(6)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j}(k_{j}-k^{\prime}_{j})+\sum_{i}(e_{i}+\boldsymbol{\delta}^{\bot}_{\mathsf{d}_{i},\bullet})

(7)

Then, we have:

\langle\Gamma^{\prime},{\mathsf{M}}^{*}_{1}\xrightarrow{{\bullet}}\cdots{\mathsf{M}}^{*}_{n}\xrightarrow{{\bullet}}{\mathsf{M}}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{1}\xrightarrow{{\mathsf{d}_{1}}}\cdots{\mathsf{M}}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{r}\xrightarrow{{\mathsf{d}_{r}}}{\mathsf{L}}_{0}\rangle\leq^{+}_{p}\langle\Gamma,{\mathsf{M}}_{1}\xrightarrow{{\bullet}}\cdots{\mathsf{M}}_{n}\xrightarrow{{\bullet}}{\mathsf{M}}^{\prime}_{1}\xrightarrow{{\bullet}}\cdots{\mathsf{M}}^{\prime}_{r}\xrightarrow{{\bullet}}{\mathsf{L}}_{0}\rangle\langle\Gamma^{\prime},\vec{x}:{\mathsf{M}}^{*},{\mathsf{M}}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{1}\xrightarrow{{\mathsf{d}_{1}}}\cdots{\mathsf{M}}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{r}\xrightarrow{{\mathsf{d}_{r}}}{\mathsf{L}}_{0}\rangle\leq^{+}_{p}\langle\Gamma,\vec{x}:\vec{{\mathsf{M}}},{\mathsf{M}}^{\prime}_{1}\xrightarrow{{\bullet}}\cdots{\mathsf{M}}^{\prime}_{r}\xrightarrow{{\bullet}}{\mathsf{L}}_{0}\rangle\lx@proof@logical@and\Gamma^{\prime},\vec{x}:{\mathsf{M}}^{*}\leq^{-}_{\sum_{j}d_{j}+\sum_{i}e^{\prime\prime}_{i}}\Gamma,\vec{x}:\vec{{\mathsf{M}}}\text{Eq. \ref{eq:context}}{\mathsf{M}}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{1}\xrightarrow{{\mathsf{d}_{1}}}\cdots{\mathsf{M}}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{r}\xrightarrow{{\mathsf{d}_{r}}}{\mathsf{L}}_{0}\leq^{+}_{\sum_{i}(\boldsymbol{\delta}^{\bot}_{\mathsf{d}_{i},\bullet}+e^{\prime}_{i})}{\mathsf{M}}^{\prime}_{1}\xrightarrow{{\bullet}}\cdots{\mathsf{M}}^{\prime}_{r}\xrightarrow{{\bullet}}{\mathsf{L}}_{0}\text{Eq. \ref{eq:type}}

This concludes the proof. ∎

. If
t\rightarrow_{{\sf R}}t^{\prime}
then
t\{x\,{:=}\,u\}\rightarrow_{{\sf R}}t^{\prime}\{x\,{:=}\,u\}
.

Terminology and Notations for the Proof

As customary in mathematical analysis, we say that a relation
{\sf P}(-)
holds for all
K\in\mathbb{N}

large enough
whenever there exists a
K^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}
such that
{\sf P}(K)
holds for all
K\geq K^{\prime}
. We also use the notation
tu^{\sim n}
for
(\cdots((tu)u)\cdots)u
(
n
times).
Also, we say that two head normal forms
h,h^{\prime}
are
spine equivalent
, written
h=_{\mathrm{sp}}h^{\prime}
, if there are
n,k\geq 0
such that:

h=\lambda x_{1}\dots x_{n}.y\,{t_{1}}\cdots t_{k}\quad\textrm{ and }\quad h^{\prime}=\lambda x_{1}\ldots x_{n}.y\,{u_{1}}\cdots u_{k}.

(8)

On closed
\lambda
-terms, the Böhm out technique amounts to applying the
tupler

{\sf T}_{n}
and the
i
-th selector
{\sf S}^{n}_{i}
defined as follows:

\begin{array}[]{rrllllllll}\textsc{$n$-tuples}&\langle t_{1},\dots,t_{n}\rangle&\coloneqq&\lambda x.xt_{1}\cdots t_{n},&\mbox{ with $x$ fresh};\\
\textsc{Tuplers}&{\sf T}_{n}&\coloneqq&\lambda x_{1}\ldots x_{n}.\langle x_{1},\dots,x_{n}\rangle;\\
\textsc{Selectors}&{\sf S}^{n}_{i}&\coloneqq&\lambda x_{1}\ldots x_{n}.x_{i},&\textrm{with }1\leq i\leq n.\end{array}

So, the tupler
{\sf T}_{n}
takes
n
arguments
t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}
and returns the tuple
\langle t_{1},\dots,t_{n}\rangle
, while the selector
{\sf S}^{n}_{i}
takes
n
arguments
t_{1},\dots,t_{n}
and returns the
i
-th argument
t_{i}
.
Note that
{\sf S}^{1}_{1}=\mathtt{I}
. Then,
{\sf T}_{n}t_{1}\cdots t_{n}u\rightarrow_{\mathtt{h}}^{*}ut_{1}\cdots t_{n}
and
{\sf S}^{n}_{i}t_{1}\cdots t_{n}\rightarrow_{\mathtt{h}}^{*}t_{i}
, whence we have the following combined
extraction property
:

\begin{array}[]{ccc}{\sf T}_{n}t_{1}\cdots t_{n}{\sf S}^{n}_{i}&\rightarrow_{\mathtt{h}}^{*}&t_{i}.\end{array}

(9)

\gettoappendix

l:separating-eta-red

Appendix 0.C
Proofs of Section
5
(Compositionality of Polarized Whiter-Cheaper)

\gettoappendix

l:repaint

Proof

By induction on the structure of the term
t
.

•

Variable, that is
t=x
.
In that case we have a derivation:

Let
\langle\Gamma^{\prime},{\mathsf{L}}^{\prime}\rangle\leq^{-}_{1}\langle\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2},{\mathsf{L}}\rangle
. Note that we can write
\Gamma^{\prime}
(not necessarily uniquely) as
\Gamma^{\prime}_{1}+\Gamma^{\prime}_{2}
such that one of the following holds:

(i)

\Gamma^{\prime}_{1}=\Gamma_{1}
,
{\mathsf{L}}^{\prime}={\mathsf{L}}
and
\Gamma^{\prime}_{2}\leq^{+}_{1}\Gamma_{2}

(ii)

\Gamma^{\prime}_{1}=\Gamma_{1}
,
\Gamma^{\prime}_{2}=\Gamma_{2}
and
{\mathsf{L}}^{\prime}\leq^{-}_{1}{\mathsf{L}}
; or

(iii)

\Gamma^{\prime}_{1}\leq^{+}_{1}\Gamma_{1}
,
\Gamma^{\prime}_{2}=\Gamma_{2}
and
{\mathsf{L}}^{\prime}={\mathsf{L}}
.

In each case, we repeatedly apply the induction hypothesis to
u
and
s
until we reach a typing that works for both:

there exists

. By the inductive hypothesis we obtain
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}_{1}\vdash^{k^{\prime}_{1}}_{\hbox to3.98pt{\vbox to3.98pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\thinspace\lower-1.9919pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}

satisfying one of:

·

\langle\Gamma^{\prime\prime}_{1},{\mathsf{M}}^{\prime}_{j}\xrightarrow{{\mathsf{c}^{\prime}}}{\mathsf{L}}^{\prime}\rangle\leq^{+}_{1}\langle\Gamma^{\prime}_{1},{\mathsf{M}}_{j}\xrightarrow{{\mathsf{c}}}{\mathsf{L}}\rangle
and
k^{\prime}_{1}=k_{1}
; or

·

\langle\Gamma^{\prime\prime}_{1},{\mathsf{M}}^{\prime}_{j}\xrightarrow{{\mathsf{c}^{\prime}}}{\mathsf{L}}^{\prime}\rangle=\langle\Gamma^{\prime}_{1},{\mathsf{M}}_{j}\xrightarrow{{\mathsf{c}}}{\mathsf{L}}\rangle
and
|k^{\prime}_{1}-k_{1}|=1
.

In the first case, note that the two typings differ in exactly one colouring, and that this cannot be between
{\mathsf{M}}^{\prime}_{j}
and
{\mathsf{M}}_{j}
by maximality of the sequence of
{\mathsf{M}}_{i}
s. Therefore
{\mathsf{M}}^{\prime}_{j}={\mathsf{M}}_{j}
. If
\mathsf{c}^{\prime}=\mathsf{c}
then we have

\langle\Gamma^{\prime\prime}_{1},{\mathsf{M}}_{j}\xrightarrow{{\mathsf{c}}}{\mathsf{L}}^{\prime}\rangle\leq^{+}_{1}\langle\Gamma^{\prime}_{1},{\mathsf{M}}_{j}\xrightarrow{{\mathsf{c}}}{\mathsf{L}}\rangle

But now notice that

|(k_{1}+k_{2}+\boldsymbol{\delta}^{\bot}_{\mathsf{c}^{\prime},\mathsf{d}})-(k_{1}+k_{2}+\boldsymbol{\delta}^{\bot}_{\mathsf{c},\mathsf{d}})|=|\boldsymbol{\delta}^{\bot}_{\mathsf{c}^{\prime},\mathsf{d}}-\boldsymbol{\delta}^{\bot}_{\mathsf{c},\mathsf{d}}|=1

completing the argument. ∎

We formally define the following commutation property for the
\leq^{-}_{1}
and
\leq^{+}_{1}
relations. The property easily generalizes to
\leq^{-}_{k}
and
\leq^{+}_{l}
for any
k
and
l
. Note that we only write down the case
k=l=1
, from which the general case follows by induction.

Lemma 12

Let
\Gamma,{{\Gamma}^{\oplus}},{{\Gamma}_{\ominus}}
and
{\mathsf{L}},{{{\mathsf{L}}}^{\oplus}},{{{\mathsf{L}}}_{\ominus}}
such that:

•

\langle{{\Gamma}_{\ominus}},{{{\mathsf{L}}}_{\ominus}}\rangle\leq^{-}_{1}\langle\Gamma,{\mathsf{L}}\rangle
and;

•

\langle{{\Gamma}^{\oplus}},{{{\mathsf{L}}}^{\oplus}}\rangle\leq^{+}_{1}\langle\Gamma,{\mathsf{L}}\rangle
.

Then there exist
{{\Gamma}^{\oplus}_{\ominus}},{{{\mathsf{L}}}^{\oplus}_{\ominus}}
such that:

•

\langle{{\Gamma}^{\oplus}_{\ominus}},{{{\mathsf{L}}}^{\oplus}_{\ominus}}\rangle\leq^{+}_{1}\langle{{\Gamma}_{\ominus}},{{{\mathsf{L}}}_{\ominus}}\rangle

•

\langle{{\Gamma}^{\oplus}_{\ominus}},{{{\mathsf{L}}}^{\oplus}_{\ominus}}\rangle\leq^{-}_{1}\langle{{\Gamma}^{\oplus}},{{{\mathsf{L}}}^{\oplus}}\rangle
